# wzrdVID Agent Log

This file is the running memory for agent work on wzrdVID. It is intentionally practical and concise: future agents should use it to understand recent decisions, verification, and unresolved risks before touching the repo.

## Required Agent Behavior

Future agents must:

- Read `AGENTS.md` before every task.
- Read `docs/agent-log.md` before every task.
- Check the latest entries before making changes.
- Append a new entry after each task that changes files or makes an important decision.
- Never delete prior log entries unless explicitly instructed.
- Keep entries factual and concise.
- Include commands/checks run.
- Include unresolved risks or follow-ups.

Entries are reverse chronological: newest entry near the top.

## 2026-08-27 - prospective v0.4.0 Zones architecture audit

- Agent/task: Codex / define the smallest truthful desktop Zones architecture without implementing it. No production UI/state/render/codec code, schema value, dependency, VERSION, signing/identity, Lite/Pages, release artifact, commit, push, tag, upload, publication, or deployment change was made. `VERSION` remains `0.3.0`; `docs/agent-impact-map.md` was intentionally left current-behavior-only.

The checkout had no `.codegraph/`, so focused read-only inspection was used without changing repository infrastructure.
- Current-pipeline finding: source selection/Stutter Hold precedes output framing; ordinary source/ANSI/text effects occupy several style-specific seams; transition then whole-frame Motion Melt/Terminal Scroll/Tape Damage/Mosaic Collapse precede the one fixed-order Material Dynamics seam (Pixel Sorting, Databending, Circuit Bending, Hex Editing, Random Noise B/W), endings, Loop, frame transport, and the post-render/pre-audio codec pass. Style begins at is the outer gate, Style FX-clean spans globally suppress optional effects and reset retained state, ANSI Coverage selects normal versus ANSI rendering without suppressing checked effects, and Layer orders complete-VOP codec writers with last writer winning.
- Accepted v1 geometry/assignment: at most three static, named, opaque-ID rectangles stored as normalized output-space `x`, `y`, `width`, and `height`; each supported effect independently selects `Full Frame` or one Zone. Coordinates rasterize to a clamped half-open output-pixel box after framing, identically for photos/video/HEIC and sequential/Random sources. No global Zones activation flag, union/exclude logic, polygons, nesting, animation, tracking, per-Zone Layer, or hidden spatial arbitration is part of v1. The proposed implementation state is schema 6 with optional `zones` and `effect_zone_assignments`; schema 3/4/5 or missing fields load Full Frame/no Zones, presets/Weird/Layer inherit the user's Zones, and Reset clears them.
- Accepted Phase 1 support: only Pixel Sorting, Databending, Circuit Bending, Hex Editing, and Random Noise B/W. Their shared post-frame seam gets one common uint8-RGB rectangular ROI helper: copy the current effect input, run the effect on a Zone-local candidate and Zone-local material/history views, then replace only that half-open slice. Effects remain sequential, so later effects see earlier overlap; `Full Frame` bypasses all Zone helpers for byte compatibility. Hard edges are intentional for v1 and feathering is deferred. The material system should reuse one lazy fitted-frame luma/motion base and cache bounded per-Zone derived analysis; absolute frame clocks and existing effect salts remain unchanged, while retained prior RGB/luma stays render-local and resets at render start and existing Style/FX boundaries.
- Ordinary-effect boundary: the remaining 16 effects are not Phase 1 claims. RGB Split, Color Drift, Scanlines, Glitch, Random Character Noise, Contrast/Saturation Boost, Tape Damage, and Mosaic Collapse can be reconsidered at their differing source/ANSI/output seams with effect-specific padding/cell/tear semantics. Motion Melt is spatially plausible only with explicit Zone-local feedback state. Ken Burns, Tunnel Zoom, Punch Zooms, VHS Wobble, Terminal Scroll Drift, Stutter Hold, and Audio Reactive Hits are global camera/source/temporal/control behaviors whose masked forms would be new semantics rather than a safe generic v1 composite.
- Codec conclusion: current DATAMOSHING, spILL!/Overflow, SKRRT, ShShSHa/Scatter, and FLOWs/Bleed handlers inject complete MPEG-4 Part 2 VOP payloads, so none can promise strict outside-Zone visual containment. General DATAMOSHING, spILL!, and FLOWs remain Zone-ineligible; post-decode masking was rejected as an inauthentic approximation. SKRRT could later build full auxiliary frames with reverse-temporal pixels only inside a Zone, and ShShSHa is the strongest candidate because its auxiliary frames already overlay multi-time spatial fragments. For a future ShShSHa prototype, the user Zone should be an allowed envelope within which existing automatic material regions are ranked/clipped, never a replacement for its two-or-more non-current temporal identities. Both candidates remain deferred until objective decoded provenance/containment, one-I/P-only auxiliary structure, exact count/clock, recovery, protection, determinism, and performance tests pass; Layer would still apply whole-VOP last-writer behavior.
- Interaction/failure/performance contract: eligibility remains `Style begins at AND not Style-FX-clean AND effect enabled`, followed by the optional spatial assignment; a clean interval is globally clean. ANSI Coverage, transitions, endings, audio, source immutability, output framing, Random coordinates, Loop protection, frame-pipe/PNG transport, and package identity stay independent. Malformed/non-finite/empty geometry is dropped; out-of-range endpoints are clipped; missing/incompatible references fail open to Full Frame with one visible warning. Frame composite/allocation failures raise `RenderError` and therefore do not trigger PNG transport fallback; future codec auxiliary failures fail closed through `DatamoshError`. Static rectangles add no persisted masks, per-pixel Python, render, main encode, or safe transcode; work stays vectorized and bounded by assigned ROI area, with at most three cached Zone analyses.
- Result/next phase: PASS. No feasibility prototype was necessary for the frame-domain Phase 1 decision, and no codec Zone support is claimed. The first implementation must remain a separately scoped five-effect frame-only phase with UI/state/migration tests, exact inside/outside and Full-Frame-oracle proof, Style/FX/ANSI/Preview/Random/Loop/audio/transport/media/package regression, source hashes, and performance evidence. A later codec phase may begin only with the ShShSHa/SKRRT auxiliary provenance prototype described above.

- Composition semantics/root decision: enabled operations execute as `DATAMOSHING -> Overflow -> SKRRT -> Scatter -> Bleed`; every handler parses the prior handler's stream and whole-VOP replacement wins at overlapping targets. General DATAMOSHING and Overflow choose source P payloads from their current input stream, so Overflow can propagate an earlier payload. SKRRT/Scatter auxiliaries are prepared from the controlled stream before ordered transforms and therefore replace, rather than byte-compose with, earlier target payloads. Bleed reads the then-current outgoing P VOP and can extend all prior corruption. Recovery anchors are mode-local untouched positions, not active writes of original bytes; another operation may target them. A later auxiliary can replace an earlier suppressed I or auxiliary P. This can change visual identity and sometimes reduce a prior mode's decoded delta, but the objective matrices found no planned target restored to controlled bytes and no lost final modified coverage. These are the same explicit sequential semantics accepted when Scatter was introduced, not a new reliability-repair regression; no product repair was made.

- Files changed: `renderer.py`, `datamosh.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, and this log. No UI control, recipe/settings schema, dependency, `VERSION`, desktop/app identity, build/package script, Lite/Apple Lite runtime, production signing, Bundle ID, Team ID, entitlement, GitHub Pages/deployment file, release artifact, tag, or published metadata changed. `AGENTS.md` and the playbook stayed unchanged because no command or agent procedure changed; the impact map was updated because the renderer-to-codec transition data flow and Circuit Bending's bounded temporal input changed.
- Transition-map architecture: after the final sequential/random `PlaybackPlan` exists, `renderer.py` derives anonymous render-time-only `DatamoshTransition` records containing local frame, absolute output frame, source kinds, and visual-transition name. No source path or persistent identifier crosses the boundary. Video/photo duration and speed mapping, random segments, preview offsets, loop seam, and Style eligibility are resolved before the tuple reaches `datamosh.py`; targets at/before the clean Style anchor are filtered out.
- Codec/keyframe strategy: the silent completed visual stream still becomes a video-only yuv420p MPEG-4 Part 2 `.m4v` with I/P VOPs only, and audio still waits until after safe CFR H.264/yuv420p validation. Encoding now forces reset opportunities at the clean Style anchor, eligible source cuts, and the protected Loop tail. Selected transition I VOPs are replaced with the prior scene's P VOP, then that prior-scene P payload is repeated across a deterministic bounded part of the incoming segment. Background events remain secondary. This is encoded prediction-stream mutation—not decoded frame blending, ghosting, displacement, Motion Melt, or raw-frame duplication.
- DATAMOSHING intensity redesign: the older generic 96-VOP proof produced Low/Medium/High at 2/3/6 suppressed arbitrary I resets plus 1/3/5 P-persistence events. The repaired transition policy covers approximately `15% + 85% * (intensity/2)` of eligible cuts and changes duration and spacing as well: Low requests 2-4 frames at stride 2, Medium 6-12 at stride 1 or 2, and High 16-30 at stride 1, always bounded by the next reset/protected tail. In the three-cut fixture this deterministically selected 1/3, 2/3, and 3/3 transitions respectively.
- Transition/VOP evidence: `/tmp/wzrdvid-visual-repair-qphs35p9/summary.json` passed. All Low/Medium/High controls began at 4 I / 44 P / 0 B / 0 S across 48 VOPs and finished at 3/45, 2/46, and 1/47. Low suppressed frame 24 and repeated one transition P position (five repeated positions total with background events); Medium suppressed frames 12/24 and repeated 6+5 transition positions (16 total); High suppressed frames 12/24/36 and repeated every frame in 13-23, 25-35, and 37-47 (40 total). Fixed-seed transition maps/stream bytes matched; changed seed changed eligible decisions while High still selected every cut.
- Visual DATAMOSHING evidence: the inspected rights-safe red-motion -> blue-motion -> green-grid -> red fixture contact sheet is `/tmp/wzrdvid-visual-repair-qphs35p9/evidence/datamosh-transition-contact-sheet.png`. It shows clean/Low/Medium/High at T-2, T-1, T, T+1, T+2, T+4, and T+8 for all three cuts. Prior-scene blocks/geometry visibly contaminate the entering scene; High is unmistakable and sustained. Summed mean-absolute deltas versus clean over sampled transition frames were Low `354.731`, Medium `665.458`, High `696.980`; High changed more than 10% of pixels at every sampled cut and commonly 100% after the first two cuts.
- Transition/source/transport coverage: the comprehensive harness passed sequential video->video, video->still HEIC, still->video, and still->still; mixed `.3gp` -> HEIC -> `.3g2`; default frame pipe; forced legacy PNG staging; synthetic pre-audio pipe failure -> PNG fallback -> DATAMOSHING; generated HEIC; all five effects plus DATAMOSHING; source audio; and CRT Flash targeting at the material boundary rather than its graphic metadata. Random assembly produced deterministic anonymous boundaries at frames 12, 19, 25, 33, and 45 across every video/photo combination; repeated runs matched transition/event maps and output SHA. Preview local frames 6/18 correctly mapped to absolute frames 12/24.
- Style/loop/failure safety: a 1.5-second Style gate retained its clean frame-18 anchor and admitted only transition targets 24/36, with no event at/before 18. Loop Friendly protects the actual renderer blend window—`ceil(min(0.75, duration/3) * fps)` tail frames—using a clean tail I anchor and no event at/after it, replacing overbroad final-GOP protection. Injected `DatamoshError` produced no output, made no PNG-fallback call, preserved sources, and removed render/prediction temp directories.
- Frame-effect repair: Pixel Sorting uses luminance/Sobel/local-contrast row/column energy to sort content-delimited runs; Databending uses source-derived row signatures, channel-order/offset reinterpretation, and edge/luma block masks; Circuit Bending uses edge/bright/motion response for sync shifts, chroma gates, bars, and bounded immediately previous styled-frame feedback; Hex Editing performs sparse content-scored chunk copy/repeat/nibble/channel/offset corruption in memory; Random Noise B/W uses local texture/edge-weighted binary thresholds and still guarantees equal RGB channels containing only 0/255. Circuit's one-frame state resets per render and at the Style boundary; all other effects remain stateless.
- Distinctness/material/determinism evidence: `/tmp/wzrdvid-style-repair-7d8j_ojj/summary.json` passed, with inspected sheets `/tmp/wzrdvid-style-repair-7d8j_ojj/style-effect-identity-low-high.png` and `/tmp/wzrdvid-style-repair-7d8j_ojj/style-material-dependence.png`. Every Low/High effect had a unique output hash and spatial-delta signature across subject/edges, smooth gradient, checker texture, dark low-detail, and moving geometry. Same seed reproduced bytes, changed seed changed damage, Low/High differed, output stayed writable RGB uint8, input arrays/hashes remained unchanged, and Random Noise remained exact binary monochrome.
- Audio/duration/output evidence: silent, source, external, and source+external cases all retained exactly 48 frames/4.000 seconds, H.264/yuv420p, video start 0, and one AAC track with start/duration 0/4.000 where enabled. Representative `.3gp`/`.3g2`, HEIC, random-assembly, Style, all-five, and packaged cases preserved source hashes. The existing HEIC/import sentinels again passed generated HEIC rendering, readable simulated protected-file internalization, unreadable rejection, and ImportedMedia cleanup protection; the 35 cached/direct ASCII glyph masks remained pixel-exact.
- Regression/UI/static result: Phase 0 transport/HEIC/glyph, Phase 1 `.3gp`/`.3g2`/Style/schema, Phase 2 direct/UI/pipeline, and Phase 3 module/pipeline/performance harnesses passed, including old schema-5/4/3 missing-key-off behavior and unchanged control order. - Performance: the revised four-second DATAMOSHING benchmark used 0.0536/0.0009/0.0987 seconds for encode/transform/safe-transcode at 960x540 and 0.0808/0.0016/0.1575 at 1280x720; the exact established Phase 3 benchmark measured total 0.282651/0.420825 seconds versus 0.271190/0.402898 in Phase 7 (+4.2%/+4.4%). - UI/state behavior: Lite now exposes the localized global `Include Source Audio` checkbox beside Add Audio, with English fallback label/help and first-load default ON. It remains page-local, is snapshotted when MAKE CLIP begins, is not mutated mid-render, and Clear Project keeps its current checked state rather than accidentally forcing it off. No persistence, upload, per-source controls, volume UI, or new permissions were added.
- Audio graph/lifecycle: one page-session `AudioContext` is requested/resumed synchronously from the MAKE CLIP gesture. Each persistent source media element owns one cached `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` through a `WeakMap` and persistent outlet gain; per-render source/Add buses feed one master `MediaStreamDestination`, so MediaRecorder receives at most one mixed audio track. Render-scoped gains/connections/controllers disconnect on completion/reset while the safe element/node ownership stays reusable. Repeated renders created one context, zero duplicate element-source errors, zero active controllers/connections afterward, and no doubled/ghost audio.
- Timeline/switching behavior: the final visual segment map is the audio authority. On each segment transition the same source and selected source timestamp are prepared before its gain rises; inactive sources remain at gain zero. Source videos use an 18 ms anti-click gain ramp, not a creative crossfade. Random assembly reuses its recorded output/source interval map exactly. Images/GIFs and videos without usable audio leave the source bus silent and do not fail the render.
- Add Audio coexistence: source OFF/no Add produces visual-only output with zero audio tracks; source ON/no Add uses the Web Audio source bus; source OFF/Add preserves the existing `captureStream()` branch where supported and the existing WKWebView MediaElementSource fallback; source ON/Add decodes the explicit Add file into the shared graph and mixes it with source audio into one track. The narrow decoded-buffer mixed path was required because iOS WKWebView stops a separate Add media element when timeline source video playback starts. Existing Add timing/looping, last-two-seconds fade, monitoring, visual analyser behavior, and native export remain intact.
- Objective synthetic evidence: Debug smoke generated local A/440 Hz and B/880 Hz moving videos, silent video C, a still, and Add/220 Hz media without committing fixtures. The final 15-second random mixed map was Still `0.000-2.982`, A source `1.551-4.952` at output `2.982-6.382`, B source `1.150-4.767` at output `6.382-9.999`, silent C source `0.498-4.141` at output `9.999-13.643`, then A source `1.071-2.428` at output `13.643-15.000`. Export PCM windows showed Add 220 throughout, 440 only in A, 880 only in B, and neither source tone in still/C. Representative FFT amplitudes were source-only `440=0.295258` with `220=0.000046` and `880=0.000027`; source-off/Add `220=0.159476` with `440=0.000758` and `880=0.000305`; mixed A `220=0.26795`, `440=0.28623`; mixed B `220=0.26779`, `880=0.29602`; mixed still/C source-tone amplitudes were effectively zero. All inspected post-cut prior-tone windows passed the `<0.025` leakage threshold outside the intentional 18 ms ramp.
- Quality/duration/output result: Fast 480p targeted 30 fps and the source-only, silent, Add-only, and full 15-second mixed modes passed. Better 720p targeted 24 fps and the repeated source-only 30-second render passed with cached source nodes, correct tone/silence sequence, and a 30.086666-second container. Audio-enabled native exports each contained exactly one H.264 video and one AAC audio track; measured containers were 1.596666 seconds source-only, 1.585 seconds Add-only, 15.086666 seconds mixed, and 30.086666 seconds repeated. ## 2026-08-25 - v0.3.0 Phase 3 authentic desktop DATAMOSHING

- Agent/task: Codex / implement desktop-only Phase 3 authentic codec/interframe DATAMOSHING on top of the validated Phase 1+2 dirty worktree; explicitly exclude frame-domain simulation, Lite/Apple Lite runtime changes, dependency additions, version/release/signing/identity/deployment changes, commits, and pushes.
- Starting baseline: after `git fetch origin`, `main`, `HEAD`, `origin/main`, and merge-base all remained at `8715d5b10ebf4c7cbc0184f659d7d85eec2abb27`; `VERSION` remained `0.2.1`. The starting seven-file Phase 1+2 diff was recorded with SHA-256 `04305ccae4551bf402725d4118f97284862479ed38aa39e1e97a255c823a5066` and was not reset, stashed, reverted, or overwritten.
- Files changed this pass: added `datamosh.py`; updated `renderer.py`, `app.py`, `app_i18n.py`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, `docs/agent-change-playbook.md`, and `docs/agent-log.md`. `VERSION`, requirements/build/release scripts, Lite/Apple Lite runtime, signing/identity, Bundle IDs, Team IDs, App Store identity, Pages deployment files, and user/imported source media were not changed.
- Phase 3A proof gate: `/tmp/wzrdvid_phase3a_proof.py` passed before any product-facing control was added. Its artifacts are under `/tmp/wzrdvid-phase3a-authentic-proof`, including `proof.json`, the 96-frame/8-second 480x270 control and low/medium/high `.m4v` plus safe H.264 outputs, decoded event frames, and `prediction-transfer-contact-sheet.png`. Visual inspection confirmed classic cross-cut prediction bleed: Scene 2 geometry remains while Scene 3 motion vectors/geometry enter after the frame-24 reset suppression. Source hashes stayed unchanged and the proof work directory cleaned.
- Codec format/parser: the controlled intermediate uses ffmpeg's native MPEG-4 Part 2 encoder as a video-only elementary `.m4v`, yuv420p, `-bf 0`, one-thread fixed/strict GOP, and no audio. The bounded linear parser preserves non-VOP headers/metadata, identifies `00 00 01 B6` units, and classifies coding type from the high two bits after the VOP start code. The 96-frame proof/control map contained exactly 8 I, 88 P, 0 B, and 0 S VOPs. No external datamosh utility, new library, NumPy simulation, raw-frame repetition, Motion Melt fallback, Python `hash()`, or unseeded randomness was used.
- Authentic transformations: selected later I-VOP units are replaced with a prior encoded P-VOP so prediction history is not reset while VOP count stays constant; selected encoded P-VOP payloads are repeated across bounded later P positions to persist/reapply prediction. Same-seed reruns produced identical event maps and manipulated elementary-stream bytes; a different seed changed the event map. Invalid/empty/truncated/no-VOP streams and unexpected B/S structures reject with `DatamoshError`.
- Intensity evidence: the direct 96-VOP matrix recorded low at 2 suppressed I resets plus 1 P-persistence event (`6 I / 90 P` afterward), medium at 3 plus 3 (`5 I / 91 P`), and high at 6 plus 5 (`2 I / 94 P`). Every case kept 96 decoded frames, 8.0 seconds, and a valid final H.264/yuv420p output. The raw manipulated elementary material can report repeated-VOP timestamp discontinuities during research decode; the product's generated-PTS/CFR safe transcode validated exact frame count/duration before audio proceeds.
- Product architecture: `renderer.py` calls the isolated `datamosh.py` stage only when DATAMOSHING is enabled, after the normal silent H.264 visual render has completed through all Phase 2 effects, transitions, endings, loop construction, and either frame transport, but before existing source/external audio extraction/mix/mux. Disabled renders add no MPEG-4 round trip. The user deliverable remains a normal MP4; source files are never overwritten and temporary `.m4v` material remains nested inside the auto-cleaned `wzrd_vid_render_*` root.
- UI/persistence: localized DATAMOSHING appears immediately before Pixel Sorting, followed by the unchanged five-effect Phase 2 order, with the requested English explainer. It shares existing Effect Intensity and Weirdness seed controls, defaults/resets off, and uses the generic schema-5 `effects` dictionary without a schema bump. `/tmp/wzrdvid_phase3_ui_harness.py` passed settings reload, schema-5 round trip, schema-5/4/3 missing-key-off compatibility, Reset Project, Reroll seed/event-map change, and unchanged Phase 2 defaults.
- Style/preview/random/loop behavior: the pre-style prefix is protected and the first styled frame is forced to an exact clean I anchor; events start only after it. Source integration passed anchors at local frame 0, mid-output frame 8, preview-crossing frame 4 with absolute frame 8, and preview-entirely-after local frame 0 with absolute offset. A boundary after the output skipped the codec stage; a two-frame eligible suffix logged no eligible event. Random assembly uses final output positions. Loop Friendly conservatively protected the final codec GOP (proof tail began at frame 84), so no event crossed the final-GOP seam. ## 2026-08-25 - v0.3.0 Phase 2 desktop frame-domain Style Stack effects

- Agent/task: Codex / implement the desktop-only Phase 2 Style Stack effects Pixel Sorting, Databending, Circuit Bending, Hex Editing, and Random Noise B/W on top of the completed Phase 1 dirty worktree; explicitly exclude Datamoshing, Lite/Apple Lite runtime work, version/release/signing/identity/deployment changes, commits, and pushes.
- Starting baseline: after `git fetch origin`, `main`, `HEAD`, `origin/main`, and merge-base all remained at `8715d5b10ebf4c7cbc0184f659d7d85eec2abb27`; `VERSION` remained `0.2.1`. The starting seven-file Phase 1 diff was recorded with SHA-256 `50e2ffae316aedaaf62b17f6cb51ab9433659affaae4e63b05a2a9def9cadf55` and was not reset, stashed, reverted, or overwritten.
- Files changed this pass: `app.py`, `app_i18n.py`, `renderer.py`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, and `docs/agent-log.md`. `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agent-change-playbook.md`, `VERSION`, requirements/build/release scripts, Lite/Apple Lite runtime, signing/identity, Bundle IDs, Team IDs, App Store identity, Pages deployment files, and user/imported source media were not changed.
- Behavior added: five localized checkbox-plus-explainer controls join the existing desktop Style Stack and share Effect Intensity plus Reroll Weirdness. All default off. In the shared styled-frame post-pipeline stage they run after the existing transition and before unchanged ending/loop handling, in fixed order: Pixel Sorting, Databending, Circuit Bending, Hex Editing, then Random Noise B/W. They therefore cover ANSI/text-art, WZRD Blocks/chunky, PUBLIC ACCESS, and normal/bypass frames without duplicating render paths; Phase 1's absolute-output `Style begins at` remains their outer gate.
- Effect implementation: Pixel Sorting performs selective bounded luminance-run sorting rather than whole-row sorting; Databending applies in-memory structured channel/data drift; Circuit Bending is explicitly software-emulated sync displacement/roll/chroma/feedback/luma/bar corruption; Hex Editing applies bounded in-memory byte-like operations without touching source files; Random Noise B/W is last and returns genuinely monochrome binary pixels before final encoding. Inputs are copied to writable contiguous RGB `uint8` arrays and source arrays are not mutated.
- Determinism and persistence: each effect derives an independent NumPy generator from the existing Weirdness seed, an explicit fixed 64-bit numeric salt, and the absolute output frame index; Python `hash()` and shared/global RNG state are not used. Reroll changes patterns without coupling effects. The existing generic schema-5 `effects` dictionary persists the flags without a schema bump; missing new keys in schema 5/4/3 load off, while existing default-on/default-off behavior is preserved. Reset Project disables all five.
- Direct visual/correctness evidence: `/tmp/wzrdvid-phase2-v030/phase2-contact-sheet.png` plus `/tmp/wzrdvid-phase2-v030/effect-validation-benchmark.json` prove disabled pixel identity, same-seed repeatability, different-seed/intensity variation, small-frame/read-only-input safety, writable `uint8` RGB output, unchanged source arrays, effect-specific invariants, and exact binary equal-channel Random Noise B/W. Individual source/no-new/effect/all-stack PNGs remain in the same temporary directory; no test media was committed.
- Performance evidence: 20-frame NumPy benchmarks at 960x540 measured Pixel Sorting 1.772 ms/frame, Databending 6.238, Circuit Bending 15.181, Hex Editing 0.101, Random Noise B/W 2.488, and the combined stack 26.894. At 1280x720 they measured 2.540, 11.093, 27.199, 0.137, 4.491, and 47.309 ms/frame respectively. No new per-pixel Python loops or dependency/executable was added; the heavier Circuit Bending path remains array-based and bounded.
- Focused settings/recipe result: `/tmp/wzrdvid_phase2_ui_harness.py` passed exact UI order/English labels/explainers, all-off defaults, normal save/reload, schema-5 round trip, schema-5/4/3 missing-key fallback, preservation of existing effect defaults/values, Reset Project, and Reroll Weirdness. `/tmp/wzrdvid_phase2_effect_harness.py` passed the direct effect matrix.

## 2026-05-23 - Protected HEIC native access investigation

- Agent/task: Codex follow-up to investigate whether desktop WZRD.VID can use Qt/macOS native file-dialog copy semantics, NSFileCoordinator, or security-scoped bookmarks to make user-selected protected HEIC/HEIF imports work more often.
- Intent: Desktop protected HEIC/HEIF import/internalization only. Preserve current import rejection fallback, renderer preflight safety, Lite, Apple Lite, website, signing, release assets, Bundle IDs, and deployment config.
- Research findings: Qt documents that `QFileDialog.getOpenFileNames()` uses the native file dialog on macOS, and PySide6 exposes `getOpenFileUrls()` but not `getOpenFileContent()`. Apple documents user-selected file access/security-scoped bookmarks as an App Sandbox entitlement model around `NSOpenPanel`/`NSSavePanel` and `startAccessingSecurityScopedResource()`. Current WZRD.VID packaging is ad-hoc signed with no sandbox/user-selected-file/bookmark entitlements, and the local Python environments do not include PyObjC `AppKit`, `Foundation`, or `objc`, so a real NSOpenPanel/NSFileCoordinator/security-scoped bookmark implementation would require a packaging/dependency/signing design outside this narrow desktop import task.
- Local copy tests: On a chmod-denied synthetic HEIC, Python `Path.read_bytes`, `shutil.copy2`, `/bin/cp`, and Qt `QFile.copy` all failed or returned false; Qt did not bypass source access denial. This supports keeping the existing import rejection when the source data itself is inaccessible.
- Files changed: `app.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, `docs/agent-log.md`.
- Behavior changed: Yes, narrowly. Protected HEIC/HEIF internalization now stages cache writes and tries `shutil.copy2`, content-only `shutil.copyfile`, and Qt `QFile.copy` before rejecting. This can recover cases where metadata-preserving copy fails but source bytes are readable. It still rejects import, adds no timeline item, and keeps renderer preflight as fallback when all copy methods fail.
- Validation: Synthetic protected-path HEIC tests covered normal metadata-preserving copy, forced `copy2` failure with content-only fallback, forced Python copy failure with Qt fallback, unreadable source failure with no partial cache file left behind, readable protected import adding from `ImportedMedia`, and unreadable protected import rejecting with the exact guidance message.
- Commands run: `git status --short --branch`; required repo docs reads; memory lookup; official Qt/Apple documentation research; local PySide/PyObjC capability checks; `build_app.sh` signing/entitlement inspection; denied-copy method probe for Python, Qt, and `/bin/cp`; `.venv/bin/python -m py_compile app.py`; synthetic protected-copy and offscreen import validation.
- Checks passed: compile passed; staged copy fallbacks copied readable HEIC bytes correctly; failed copies left no partial cache artifacts; unreadable protected HEIC still rejected and did not add to the timeline.
- Checks failed: None.
- Known gaps: No real macOS Messages/Photos TCC-denied user-selected file was available, and no sandboxed/notarized build with user-selected-file/bookmark entitlements was created. Security-scoped bookmark support remains a future packaging/signing design, not a safe narrow patch here.
- Next recommended prompt: Build a sandboxed/notarized macOS test harness with user-selected-file read entitlement and PyObjC/AppKit access, then compare NSOpenPanel URL copy, security-scoped bookmark resolution, and child ffmpeg access on real Messages/Photos HEIC selections.

## 2026-05-23 - v0.2.1 desktop Glitch Hell render slowdown investigation and fix

- Agent/task: Codex / investigate and fix the WZRD.VID v0.2.1 desktop renderer slowdown reported from a real support log.
- Intent: Desktop renderer performance only. Preserve visual output, Lite, Apple Lite, website, signing, release assets, and deployment config.
- Files changed this pass: `renderer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, `docs/agent-log.md`.
- Behavior changed: Yes. ASCII ANSI/text presets now use a per-render glyph-mask cache for drawing text-art frames, avoiding repeated glyph rasterization while preserving ASCII output pixels. Chunky/Unicode glyph rendering remains on the direct text path. Render logs now include more targeted hot-path timing.
- Reproduction media: generated synthetic local media under `/tmp/wzrdvid-v021-render-slowdown-synth` with 75 timeline items: 52 JPG, 11 HEIC/HEIF, 12 MOV, plus external AAC audio and worky mode. No generated media was committed.
- Baseline before fix: 540x304/18fps Glitch Hell cold-cache run rendered 175 frames in 44.11s; frame timing was source/still 6.53s, ANSI prep 2.26s, text render 34.69s. 1280x720/24fps cold-cache run rendered 234 frames in 91.61s; frame timing was source/still 10.55s, ANSI prep 16.60s, text render 61.87s. Full Quality still proxies reached 2560x1920 on the synthetic 4:3 stills.
- After fix: same 540x304/18fps run completed in 16.13s with text render 6.61s. Same 1280x720/24fps run completed in 44.80s with text render 15.50s. The new hot-path timing split showed the 1280x720 run at resize/framing 5.41s, ANSI prep effects 10.89s, text sample/luma 0.67s, ImageDraw.text/glyph draw 10.44s, ANSI output effects 4.10s.
- Findings: per-frame still reloading was not reproduced; each still loaded once per render. Cold HEIC proxy misses were expected because the cache was isolated and proxy keys differ by output proxy size. Excessive 2560x1920 still proxies amplify Full Quality source/framing cost, but the largest safe fix was text glyph caching because it preserves ASCII pixels. No unintended bypass/text-render behavior was found in the full-ANSI repro.
- Validation: direct pixel comparison confirmed cached ASCII glyph-mask rendering matched the old direct `ImageDraw.text` path for a representative Glitch Hell frame. Tiny optimized render smoke produced `/tmp/wzrdvid-v021-render-slowdown-synth/tiny_smoke_optimized_29mb.mp4`; ffprobe confirmed H.264/yuv420p video and AAC audio for the after benchmark outputs and the tiny smoke.
- Commands run: required repo docs reads; memory lookup; `git status --short --branch`; renderer/app/preset/performance-note inspections with `rg`, `sed`, and `nl`; synthetic media generation with `.venv/bin/python`, `ffmpeg`, and `magick`; before/after render timing harnesses with isolated `WZRDVID_STILL_CACHE_DIR`; direct Glitch Hell pixel-equality check; tiny optimized render smoke; `.venv/bin/python -m py_compile app.py app_i18n.py renderer.py ffmpeg_utils.py presets.py theme.py run.py`; ffprobe video/audio checks.
- Checks passed: Python compile passed; direct glyph-mask pixel equality passed; tiny render smoke passed; ffprobe confirmed valid H.264/yuv420p MP4s at 540x304/18fps and 1280x720/24fps plus AAC audio; optimization smoke stayed under 29 MB.
- Checks failed: initial multi-file ffprobe invocation used an invalid ffprobe command shape and was rerun successfully as a per-file loop.
- Known gaps: Did not run the user's exact private media or the full 3360-frame support timeline. Still proxy dimensions remain intentionally unchanged in this pass to avoid visual-quality risk without a separate guarded setting or broader quality review. An unrelated untracked `docs/agent-system-audit.md` appeared after the initial clean baseline and was left untouched.

## 2026-05-14 - Desktop PUBLIC ACCESS read-only array crash fix

- Agent/task: Codex / fix the v0.2.0 desktop preview/render crash `ValueError: assignment destination is read-only` reported by a public v0.2.0 user with JPEG stills, PUBLIC ACCESS, Smart Portrait framing, random transitions, CRT Shutdown + loop-friendly, and both frame pipe and PNG fallback.
- Intent: Desktop renderer crash fix only. Preserve Lite, Apple Lite, website, packaging, versioning, DUNS/App Store metadata, GitHub Pages config, release metadata, and unrelated files.
- Files changed this pass: `renderer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/agent-log.md`.
- Behavior changed: Yes, crash handling for ANSI/PUBLIC ACCESS output effects. Visual output is intended to stay the same; the renderer now makes effect-frame arrays writable before in-place scanline/RGB/glitch mutations when Pillow/NumPy returns a read-only view.
- Root cause: `_apply_ansi_output_effects()` converted the scanline-processed PIL image back with `np.asarray(..., dtype=np.uint8)`. That array can be read-only, and later in-place glitch/RGB writes could raise `ValueError: assignment destination is read-only`. Frame pipe was not the root cause because PNG fallback uses the same shared frame/effect path.
- Validation result: direct writable-array guard smoke passed for a read-only scanline array. Synthetic JPEG still renders passed for PUBLIC ACCESS + Smart Portrait + Random transition + CRT Shutdown + loop-friendly with default frame pipe using Pocket Camera dither, Newspaper halftone dither, and no dither; the Pocket Camera case also passed with forced legacy PNG staging. A Classic ANSI video+still pipe smoke also passed. ffprobe confirmed valid H.264/yuv420p MP4s with expected short durations and AAC where external audio was enabled.
- Commands/tools run: required repo docs reads; memory lookup; `git status --short --branch`; `git log --oneline --decorate -12`; targeted `rg`/`nl` inspections around `_render_frames`, `render_text_art_frame`, `_apply_ansi_output_effects`, PUBLIC ACCESS, dither, and NumPy array conversions; direct read-only-array smoke; synthetic JPEG render matrix under `/tmp/wzrdvid-readonly-crash-smoke`; ffprobe validation.
- Known gaps: Did not rerun the user's exact nine private JPEGs or rebuild/repackage the public v0.2.0 app in this pass. This fix should be included in the next desktop patch package after validation.
- Next recommended prompt: Rebuild/package a v0.2.0 desktop hotfix after the PUBLIC ACCESS read-only array crash fix, then verify the user’s JPEG preview/render scenario from the packaged app.

Safari WebDriver and Apple Events JavaScript automation were blocked by local Safari settings. The 8-file import loaded 8/8 files, failed 0, logged 8 HEIC/HEIF files, and completed in 0.05s with per-file HEIC decode logs mostly 0.00-0.01s. A 30s random clip render with added audio completed to MP4-ready state. Clear Project removed selected media, audio, rendered output state, reset download state, and allowed reselecting the same HEIC batch while keeping language/duration/quality/ANSI/random preferences.
- Safari memory observation: total Safari/WebKit RSS was about 596 MB after import and about 1.05 GB during/after render; the largest WebContent process was about 348 MB. No freeze or obvious runaway memory behavior was observed for this 8-file batch.
- Apple Lite WKWebView results: used a temporary copy at `/tmp/wzrdvid-apple-heic-profile` with a debug-only smoke harness embedding the same 8 real HEIC files, leaving tracked Apple Lite files untouched. On the iPhone 17 simulator, WKWebView loaded 8/8 HEIC files, failed 0, logged import in 0.66s, and per-file HEIC decode was about 0.07-0.13s. Render start delay was 86 ms; a 15s random HEIC render completed in 15.19s with MP4/H.264 video, Web Audio audio, 451/450 frames, 8 timeline sources, and a 12.19 MB blob. Clear Project reset runtime state and allowed the same HEIC batch to be reselected.
- Proxy decision: do not add a Lite still-proxy cache now. On the 64-column 720x406/24fps synthetic matrix, Amber Terminal 10s took 24.41s total with 23.48s in frame render, 16.03s in `render_text_art_frame()`, and 12.18s in `ImageDraw.text()`. Amber Terminal 30s scaled linearly at 73.95s total. PNG writes and ffmpeg encode were secondary, and worky audio mux was about 0.06s.
- Optimization decision: Skipping literal-space glyph draws was the only tiny low-risk code change found, but the after-run was not faster on the test matrix, so it was reverted and no renderer code change was kept.
- Known gaps: Major speedup likely needs a v0.3.0 renderer refactor such as direct ffmpeg frame piping and/or batched/vectorized text drawing. The user's real long render should still be rerun after the hue-overflow fix to validate failure behavior and actual wall time.

- Intent: Move Apple Lite from source-only wrapper groundwork to a simulator-ready local Xcode project while preserving the bundled local Lite shell, no-upload/no-network stance, desktop performance freeze, and no publish/push/tag/package boundary.
- Files changed this pass: `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `apple-lite/README.md`, `apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, `apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/WZRDVIDLite.xcscheme`, `apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite/App/LiteSmokeHarness.swift`, `apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite/App/LiteWebView.swift`, `apple-lite/scripts/run_simulator_smoke.py`, `docs/APPLE_LITE_APP_RESEARCH.md`, `docs/agent-change-playbook.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, `docs/agent-log.md`.
- Behavior changed: Yes, Apple Lite groundwork only. A simulator-ready Xcode project now builds the existing SwiftUI/WKWebView shell, and Debug builds can run a dormant smoke harness when launched with `WZRDVID_LITE_SMOKE=1` or `--lite-smoke`. Desktop renderer/performance code, desktop UI, static Lite browser behavior, website behavior, release packaging, version files, publishing, pushing, and tagging were not changed.
- Commands run: `git status --short --branch`; `git log --oneline -12`; required repo docs reads; Apple Lite source/project inspection; `xcodebuild -list -project apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite.xcodeproj`; `python3 -m py_compile apple-lite/scripts/prepare_lite_web_bundle.py apple-lite/scripts/run_simulator_smoke.py`; `plutil -lint apple-lite/WZRDVIDLite/App/Info.plist`; `python3 apple-lite/scripts/run_simulator_smoke.py`; full Python compile command including Apple Lite scripts; `node --check docs/i18n.js`; `node --check docs/lite/app.js`; Lite forbidden-network grep across `docs/lite`, `docs/index.html`, `docs/i18n.js`, and `apple-lite`; `git diff --check`; final status checks.
- Checks passed: Xcode 26.3 recognized the committed project target and shared scheme. The simulator smoke built the project with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`, installed it on the iPhone 17 iOS 26.2 simulator, launched the local bundled WKWebView shell, and reported passing checks for Lite load, local file input surface, synthetic local image import through the Lite code path, Spanish language switching, 15-second duration, Random clip assembly, MediaRecorder/canvas capture, blob export surface, and generated `wzrdvid-lite-15s` download readiness. Python and JavaScript syntax checks passed, Info.plist lint passed, forbidden-network grep returned no matches, and diff whitespace check passed.
- Checks failed: The first hand-written project file had one bad Sources build-phase reference; fixed before the final project read/build. The first smoke harness used `evaluateJavaScript` for an async result and returned an unsupported-type error; fixed by using `WKWebView.callAsyncJavaScript`.
- Decisions made: Kept the smoke harness Debug-only and environment/argument gated so normal app launches do not run tests. Kept generated `LiteWeb/`, `DerivedData/`, and Python cache output ignored and removed after validation.
- Known gaps: The smoke uses a synthetic image file to exercise the local file import code path; it does not manually drive the iOS document/photo picker with real user media. Native share sheet/export bridge, final Team ID, production Bundle ID, App Store Connect record, TestFlight build, and real-device smoke remain future work.
- Next recommended prompt: Manually test WZRD.VID Lite on a real iPhone/iPad with local videos/photos, then decide whether a native import/share bridge is needed before TestFlight setup.

## 2026-05-10 - v0.2.0 performance hardening and Apple Lite groundwork

- Agent/task: Codex / v0.2.0 performance audit, conservative optimization pass, long-media stress smoke, and Apple Lite app groundwork research.
- Intent: Treat v0.2.0 as performance hardening plus Apple/Lite rollout preparation by auditing long-media risks first, then making only low-risk measured changes and documenting Apple app options without publishing, pushing, tagging, packaging, or adding UI/effects features.
- Files changed this pass: `ffmpeg_utils.py`, `renderer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/PERFORMANCE_NOTES.md`, `docs/APPLE_LITE_APP_RESEARCH.md`, `docs/agent-impact-map.md`, `docs/agent-log.md`.
- Behavior changed: Yes, performance/logging only. ffprobe metadata is cached in-process by resolved path, mtime, and size; render logs now include stage timing; 30+ minute source video/audio inputs emit long-media warnings. Visual output, recipe schema, Lite behavior, deployment config, packaging, publishing, pushing, and tagging were not changed.
- Commands run: `git status --short --branch`; `git log --oneline -12`; required repo docs reads; performance audit greps/reads across `app.py`, `renderer.py`, `ffmpeg_utils.py`, README, changelog, and prior logs; full Python compile command; `node --check docs/i18n.js`; `node --check docs/lite/app.js`; synthetic media generation and render stress smokes with ffmpeg/ffprobe using the repo `.venv`; probe-cache smoke; official Apple Developer docs research for enrollment, App Review, privacy, App Store Connect, SDK submission requirements, and WKWebView local-file loading; `git diff --check`.
- Checks passed: Python and JavaScript syntax checks passed before stress. Probe cache smoke showed repeated duration/audio/video helper calls for the same file issued 1 ffprobe call total. Synthetic stress smokes passed for baseline short render, long source to 10s, long source to 90s, long source plus random/photo, source-audio only, long external audio only, long external audio with worky mode, external plus selected source audio, random plus source audio, random plus match-to-music rejection, and 5s/10s preview-like renders. Outputs were verified with ffprobe for duration/audio presence where applicable.
- Checks failed: The first stress attempt used system Python and failed because `cv2` was not installed there; reran with the repo `.venv` and passed.
- Decisions made: Added conservative runtime probe caching and observability instead of rewriting frame seeking, changing ffmpeg command structure, changing the worky profile, changing recipe schema, or touching Lite. Apple app work remains research-only until Developer/D-U-N-S setup is complete.
- Known gaps: Real 4K/60fps, variable-frame-rate, long-GOP, and difficult phone footage still need beta testing. OpenCV timestamp seeking remains the likely long-video/random-mode bottleneck. PNG frame staging can still grow with long/high-FPS outputs. Source-audio random assembly can still get expensive with many short segments. Apple Lite app implementation is intentionally not scaffolded yet.
- Next recommended prompt: Run a v0.2.0 beta media pass with real long phone footage, then start WZRD.VID Lite Apple app packaging only after no desktop performance blockers remain.

## Entry Template

```markdown
## YYYY-MM-DD - Short title

- Agent/task:
- Intent:
- Files changed:
- Behavior changed:
- Commands run:
- Checks passed:
- Checks failed:
- Decisions made:
- Known gaps:
- Next recommended prompt:
```

## Rules for Log Entries

- Use reverse chronological order, newest entry near the top.
- Do not include secrets.
- Do not paste huge logs.
- Link or name relevant files.
- Mention if deployment config, GitHub Pages, or build output was touched.
- Mention if generated files were intentionally avoided.
- Keep entries factual and concise.
